How to play (the in-game guide)

The in-game guide (H), page by page, as a keyboard player reads it.

First steps

Everyone begins alone on a small island. This is the loop of the whole game.

  1. Use WASD to walk. Hold Space (or the mouse button) beside a tree, rock or berry bush to harvest it. What drops flies into your pockets.
  2. Press B (Build) and place a Workbench (6 wood). Chests, campfires and garden beds are in the same menu. Press C for Crafting: rope and bait by hand, most other things at a station.
  3. The quest tracker at the top left always shows your next steps. Hover a line to see how to do it; press J for the whole Journal.
  4. Every level gives a skill point. Press K and spend it: tools, recipes and buildings unlock through the skill tree.
  5. When the sun sets, monsters come out. Light a campfire and stay near it, or fight back with your Wooden Club (hotbar slot 2).
  6. Walk to the edge of your island and press E to buy land. Keep going and your island joins up with your friends’ islands.
  7. In the last minute of the day, gather at a Campfire or Table with a friend, or with your companion beside you. A ring fills over the fire, and then you all have Hearthside until dawn: hearts and energy mend faster, +15% XP.
Tips
  • The game saves by itself. In a solo world, time stops while a window is open.
  • Forgot a key? Esc opens the menu, and its Controls tab lists every key.

Farming

Garden beds grow wheat, carrots, pumpkins and more. You can walk straight over a bed.

  1. Build a Garden Bed (B, Farm tab) for 3 wood. Make a few: crops are your steady income.
  2. Get seeds: break wildflowers and berry bushes, they drop wheat, carrot, beet, corn and more. Right-click a seed in your Backpack (I) to pin it to the hotbar.
  3. Select the seed on your hotbar, stand by an empty bed and press E to plant it. With no seed selected, E lets you choose one from a list.
  4. Wait. Crops grow on their own, faster in the rain and in the right season. A companion with a Farm task also waters them.
  5. When the crop is ripe (full-size with fruit), press E again to harvest. You get food and often your seed back.
  6. Sell crops at a Market Stall, cook them at a Kitchen, or grind wheat into flour at a Millstone. A little of your harvest becomes seed for the next round.
  7. A Weather Vane (Build, Decor) shows the next three days: rain (crops grow up to 50% faster in it), fog, and the nights to come, such as the Blood Moon, meteors and fairies. press E on it.
Tips
  • Give a creature the Farm task (P, then click it) and it harvests, replants and waters while you do something else.
  • Beds sit under you, so walking through your garden never gets stuck.

Fishing

Quiet, relaxing, and you can never lose anything but the fish.

  1. Get a Fishing Rod: press C, open the Workbench tab and craft one (4 wood and 3 rope; rope is 2 fiber, made by hand).
  2. Walk to a shore and face open water. Press Q to cast: the bobber lands about 2 to 7 tiles out. “Too close” or “Too far” just means step back or forward.
  3. Wait for a bite. The bobber ripples while the fish thinks. Stay close: if you walk away the line goes slack.
  4. When the bobber dips and a gold ! pops up, press Q straight away to hook it. Too early and it is gone; you have about a second.
  5. Big fish fight back. After “Hooked!” answer every tug (a blue !) with Q again. Miss or jump the gun twice and it gets away.
  6. Bait helps: craft it by hand (2 fiber + a berry for 2) or from minnows at the Workbench. One bait is used per cast; bites come sooner and rare fish get likelier.
  7. Better rods give more time and rarer fish: the Steel Rod at an Anvil, the Crystal Rod once you know Smithing III.
Tips
  • Different fish like different places and times: eels and lanterns at night, trout in cold daylight, koi only in the rain. The Fish tab in your Journal hints at each one.
  • Pearls and odd junk turn up too. Sell fish at a Market Stall.

Crafting

Almost everything is made at a station. Stand next to it and press C.

  1. Press C. With no station nearby you can still make rope and bait by hand. Everything else needs a station.
  2. Build a station from the Build menu (B, Craft tab): Workbench first, then Anvil, Kitchen, Loom and Alchemy Table as the skill tree unlocks them. A station shows up in the Crafting window once you can use it.
  3. Press E on a station (or press C beside it). The window lists what it can make; click a recipe and then Craft.
  4. Pick a recipe to see what it needs: ingredients you lack show in red. A padlock on a recipe means you still have to unlock it in the skill tree.
  5. You do not have to carry everything: crafting also uses the chests within eight tiles of you (pockets first). Put a Chest beside your Workbench and keep it stocked.
  6. Use the search box at the top (or press /) to find a recipe by what it makes or what it needs, across every station.
  7. Machines like the Furnace and Sawmill take ingredients and work on their own. Put things in with E; they carry on while you are away.
Tips
  • Ore does not become bars by itself: smelt it in a Furnace (coal, wood or peat as fuel).
  • Bait, ropes and planks are cheap: make a stack of them while you wait for crops.

Building a house

Lay a floor, raise walls, add a doorway and a roof. Nothing spawns inside, and monsters cannot get in.

  1. Open the Build menu (B): floors are the first things in the Floors & walls tab (Plank, Stone Path, Brick, Slate, Carpet). Drag to lay a whole row at once.
  2. In the same Floors & walls tab pick a wall: wood, stone, brick, or a wall with a window. Drag along the edge of the floor; walls join up with their neighbours by themselves.
  3. Add a Doorway: leave a gap, or place it straight onto a piece of wall and it takes that piece’s place, turned to fit. You walk through it; monsters cannot.
  4. Put in a Garden Bed, a Chest, a Campfire or a Table, whatever you want inside. You keep placing the same thing until you right-click or press Esc, so a whole row of beds is quick.
  5. Finish with a roof: Thatch, Tile or Slate. Drag across the whole room. The roof fades away when you stand under it, so you can see inside.
  6. To change your mind, point at a wall, floor or anything else (or stand close) and hold X to take it down: the piece is outlined while you hold. You get 60% of its materials back.
  7. Set a Table (Build, Decor) before a boss and let everyone put out a dish with a buff: four different ones, eight portions each. press E at it to eat a portion of every dish at once, each buff marked with its cook. E again to set out or take back your own.
Tips
  • Nothing is ever born on a floor, and nothing is born inside a closed room or yard (walls and doorways all the way round). Flying monsters cannot cross walls, doorways or roofs either.
  • Blueprints (V) copy a whole room and paste it anywhere.

Backpack & hotbar

You can carry up to 999 of each item, and a pack lets you carry even more.

  1. Press I for your Backpack. The doll on the left shows what you wear, and your outfit appears on your character in the world too.
  2. Every item stacks up to 999 (the limit is shown at the bottom). When a stack is full, you stop picking that item up. Everything else still works.
  3. Make a pack: a Satchel and a Rucksack at the Workbench, an Explorer’s Pack and a Hauler’s Frame at the Anvil, a Rift Pack at the Rift Forge. Each adds more room for every item.
  4. Equip a pack by clicking it in the Backpack. It goes in the bag slot on the doll (bottom right) and shows on your back in the world.
  5. Right-click an item to pin it to the hotbar (slots 1 to 8). Press 1 to 8, or scroll the mouse wheel, to switch. Tools equip, food and potions are used, seeds and pods are held.
  6. Lost in a big pile? Type in the search box (or press /) to filter items. Chests, crafting, the build menu and the market have one too.
  7. Label your chests: open one and press Sort & label. Choose what it takes (all ores, only stone, just seeds, single items) and the icon it wears. It refuses anything else, and inserters and sorting creatures treat it as the home of exactly that.
Tips
  • Gear (swords, armour) stacks to 9 whatever pack you wear.
  • If you fall and wake up at home, your things wait where you fell in a Lost Backpack. Walk back (it glows at night) and press E.

Creatures & helpers

Tame wild creatures, take one along, and give it a job.

  1. Learn Taming in the skill tree (K), then craft pods at the Workbench (planks, fiber and a slime gel).
  2. Find a wild creature (they wander the islands, some only at night), select a pod on your hotbar and press T when you are close. Rare creatures are harder to catch; Great and Ultra pods help.
  3. Press P to see your roster. Click a creature to bring it along: it follows you and fights beside you.
  4. Give your companion a job. Click one of its task buttons in the P window, or on its card at the top left: Gather (trees), Mine (rocks), Farm (crops) or Guard (monsters). What it collects goes into your pockets.
  5. Only creatures that are good at a job can do it: the card tells you which. Working makes them level up faster.
  6. To leave a creature working while you do other things, press Give a job… on its card (see Creatures at work). A Creature Den works too: the creatures you leave there work the land around it. Treats keep them happy.
  7. Stand next to your companion and press E to pet it. Hearts float up and it grows fonder (five pets a day count). A Fond creature digs up a small gift for you once a day, and a Devoted one finds better things.
Tips
  • Two creatures and treats in a Hatchery make an egg, and the child inherits the best traits.
  • Creatures that work for you level up, and teach you too: a share of the XP they earn on a job comes to you, even while you are away.

Creatures at work

Give a creature a standing job: look after an island, or run a furnace or a workbench for you.

  1. Press P, click a creature and press Give a job…. It leaves your side, goes to its post and keeps working, even while you are away. You can have four at work at once (the skill tree adds more).
  2. Work an island: pick an island you own on the little map, then a job: Lumber, Mining, Farming, Hauling, Sorting or Guarding. A sorter walks between your chests and puts like with like. One creature, one job.
  3. Island workers carry what they get to a Chest near the middle of that island (within nine tiles). Farmers fetch seeds from the chest, plant them in your Garden Beds, harvest and carry the crops back. No chest, or a full one: a bubble says so.
  4. Run a machine: open a Furnace, Sawmill, Millstone, Anvil, Kitchen or Assembler (E) and press Put a creature to work. The keeper loads ingredients and fuel from the chests within nine tiles, empties the machine into them, and makes it run faster.
  5. Run a workshop: do the same at a Workbench, Loom or Alchemy table, then pick a recipe in the Craft window (C), choose how many and press Order. The worker takes the ingredients from your chests, makes them one by one and puts them back.
  6. Press P and open the Workforce tab to see everyone at work, how it is going and how much they have made. Change a job or send a creature home from there.
Tips
  • A creature with a bubble over its head needs help: Workforce says what (a chest, fuel, ingredients, power).
  • A creature at work shows what it is doing: an icon over its head and, when you are close, a caption. The more dots it has in a job, the faster it works.

Luck & loot

Crates, a Fortune Wheel, buried treasure and little strokes of luck. Press your luck if you dare.

  1. Crates drop from monsters now and then, from bosses and expeditions, and come as quest rewards. Click a crate in your Backpack (I) to open it. Wooden, Silver, Golden, Mythic: the better the crate, the more and the rarer the loot.
  2. Silver crates and better never go long without something epic: after a run of misses the next one is guaranteed to hold it. Luck skills, charms and the Lucky buff make rare finds likelier.
  3. While you chop, mine or pick, a Lucky Find sometimes pops out: coins, a useful item, even a crate. Now and then a tree or rock shines gold. Break it for triple drops and a prize on top.
  4. Buried treasure: a mound with a red X appears on your islands from time to time. Break it like a rock. Bottles from fishing, crates and treasure hold old letters, and each one shows you a mound.
  5. Build a Fortune Wheel (B, Special). The first spin each day is free, more cost coins, a little more each time. Prizes are coins, bundles, crates, and a tiny JACKPOT wedge that gets likelier the longer it misses.
  6. After a coin prize you can press your luck: Double or nothing risks it for a 45% chance to double it, up to four times in a row. Or keep it. Collect Lantern Shards along the way: they are the key to the story.
  7. With friends, a Great Oak or Titan Boulder now and then grows on your islands. It only takes damage while two farmers have swung at it within three seconds. Everyone who helps gets their own pile, and more with every extra hand.
Tips
  • Crates in your Backpack can wait: open them when you have a minute, the fanfare is half the fun.
  • The day, the night and the weather never change the odds: only your luck does.

Fighting & nights

Nights are dangerous, but easy on a new farmer and they grow with you.

  1. Hold Space (or the mouse button) to swing the weapon you have equipped. Slimes and bats are gentle; the monsters at night get harder as you and your party level up.
  2. Dodge by walking away from a monster’s windup. With the Dash skill, Shift gives you a quick burst: dash through a hit at the last moment for a Perfect dash (your energy back, and your next hit is a critical one).
  3. Eat to heal: press F for the best food you carry, or use a hotbar slot. A campfire also heals you at night.
  4. Armour and charms lower the damage you take. Craft better ones at the Anvil, Loom and Alchemy Table, and equip them in the Backpack.
  5. If your hearts run out you are down. A friend can stand next to you and hold E to pick you up and you lose nothing. Wake up at home instead and half your XP towards the next level is gone, and your whole backpack stays where you fell.
  6. On your own, or tired of waiting, press R (or click the button) and you wake up at home straight away.
  7. Bosses have attacks that need friends. Frozen: a friend holds E beside you to thaw you. Hexed: stand next to a friend and the curse jumps to them. Chained: stay within 7 tiles of your partner. A lone farmer never meets them.
Tips
  • The six bosses are called at the Boss Altar with sigils. Bring friends and bring food.
  • A campfire or lantern keeps monsters off the tiles around it.

Quests & journal

The Journal tells a story, offers side quests and keeps track of everything you found.

  1. Press J. The Story tab has chapters; finish the steps and the chapter is claimed for you with a reward.
  2. Hover a quest line, in the tracker or the Journal, to see how to do it. Steps that need a key press name the key.
  3. Side quests are in the Quests tab: pick one, read what it asks, and press Accept quest. It counts from the moment you take it. You can have a few at once.
  4. Press Pin to screen on a quest to keep it in the tracker at the top left; press it again to unpin.
  5. Daily bounties change every day and pay coins, skill points and items. Medals and the fish and creature logs fill up as you play.
  6. The Chronicle tab is the story of your farm, written by the game as it happens: islands raised, bosses felled, chapters finished, nights survived. Everybody on the farm shares it.
  7. On the first morning of each season after the first, the Season wish window opens. The farm votes for one of three blessings (faster crops, cheaper building, more XP) that helps everyone until the season ends.
Tips
  • A quest never takes anything from you. If you already have the item, it still counts.
  • The tracker follows what you pinned, then the story.

The caves below

A second world under the first: rock to dig, ore in it, treasure and things in the dark.

  1. Learn Mine Shafts (K, Gathering branch), then build a Mine Shaft from the Special tab of the Build menu (B) and press E on it to climb down. The caves are as big as the world and line up with it: a shaft opens right under where you built it.
  2. Down there it is dark and the rock is solid. Hold the action key (or your finger) beside rock to dig it with your pick. Stone comes out, and coal, iron, copper and gold hide in veins. Crystal needs a gold pick.
  3. Everything you dig stays dug, so you shape the caves as you go. Chests, campfires, lanterns, walls and floors all work down there too.
  4. Creatures of the dark come for whoever digs: more of them, and nastier, the nearer you get to the edge of the map. If you fall, you wake up at home and your backpack stays where you fell, so go back with friends.
  5. Treasure chests, crystal and mushrooms are scattered through the caves. Far out are ancient chambers: square rooms with a vault, watched by elite guardians until you open it. The corner map shows the rock round you, ore in colour.
  6. Every shaft has a ladder at the bottom: press E on it to climb back up. If the shaft is taken down, the ladder goes with it and anyone below is brought up.
Tips
  • Gold and crystal are rare near the middle of the map and common near the edge.
  • A lantern lights your way down and the way back.

The Dread Reaches

Four dark blocks of haunted land out in the wilds, each guarded by a warden. Bring friends.

  1. Open the map (Esc): the dark violet blocks with a skull, far out near the corners of the world, are the Dread Reaches. Nobody owns them and nobody can buy them: buy the land beside them and you can walk in.
  2. The dead haunt it day and night, tougher than the land around, and more of them the more farmers are inside. Bring armour, potions and a campfire. They fade away again half a minute after the last farmer leaves.
  3. At the heart of each block sleeps a warden: one of the altar bosses, with twice the health and more bite. His health bar shows when you are near, and he never leaves his post.
  4. Everything in the Reaches is richer: more ore, a treasure chest in every outer patch and a vault by the warden. Build a base on the edge, a floor and walls, and go in and out.
  5. Beat a warden with your party and you get all the usual boss spoils plus gold crates and rift shards. He wakes again five days later, so the hoard comes back.
Tips
  • It is dim and violet in there even at noon. A lantern or campfire shows you what is coming.
  • If you fall in the Reaches you drop your backpack there: go back for it with friends.

The Blight & raids

Dark nest isles out at sea send raids at night. Defend your base, then go and break the nests.

  1. The red marks out at sea on the map are Blight nests, each on a dark isle nobody may buy while its nest lives. A nest within 6 plots of your base (your home, or a campfire on your land) sends part of the night as a raid.
  2. At dusk a banner says where the raid gathers. Raiders wade across the sea and march on your base; walls and a doorway stop them, so wall your base in and fight them at the walls.
  3. To reach one, buy land toward it. Then hit the nest with your weapon. A nest that is struck wakes its brood, so bring friends, food and potions.
  4. A nest grows a level every night: more health, nastier brood, better spoils. Do not wait too long. Breaking it pays coins, XP and Blight Cores to everyone who fought.
  5. Nests spread to the sea beside them, and two of a kind side by side may merge into one nest with both levels added. A broken nest’s isle is land you can buy.
  6. Build a Bed (Floors & walls) in an outpost near the nests and press E on it: after a fall you wake up there instead of at home.
Tips
  • The kind of a nest (Bone, Swarm, Beast or Spirit) says which monsters it hatches.

Friends & online

Up to sixteen farmers share one world and one farm.

  1. Everyone starts on their own island, far apart. Buy land towards each other until the islands connect.
  2. Press Enter to chat, G then 1 to 8 for an emote, and middle-click (or Alt+click) to ping a spot on the map.
  3. When a friend goes down, walk up and hold E until they get up. They are back with a couple of hearts.
  4. When you go down, wait for a friend to hold E next to you, or press R to wake up at home. That costs half your XP towards the next level, and your backpack stays where you fell (a Lost Backpack: anybody can fetch things out of it).
  5. Chests, machines and the Market Stall are shared: anyone on the farm can use them. Build a Mailbox and friends can leave you parcels and notes (press E at theirs to leave one); a postcard with a small present comes every morning.
  6. The host keeps the world saved. Come back later and everything, including your character, is where you left it.
  7. Pick a name and a secret word when you join. Use the same two on any phone or computer and you are the same farmer, with everything you own. Never share the word.
Tips
  • Open the Farmers tab in the menu (Esc) to see what everyone wears.
  • The server’s link (and password, if it has one) is all a friend needs to join.

Automation: start here

Machines can work for you round the clock. This page gets your first little production line running.

  1. Learn the skills first (K, the Industry branch): Logistics for belts and inserters, Electricity for poles and turbines, Mining Machines for drills. Each is a single point.
  2. Press V and open Starter layouts: a Smelter line, a Flour mill, a Mining outpost and more, each ready to place with one click. Pick one, press Place it, aim the green ghost and click. R turns it.
  3. Fill the first chest (E): iron ore and a little coal for the Smelter line. Inserters carry it into the furnace and the bars out into the second chest. Open that chest to collect them.
  4. Read the little badge over each machine: a green gear means working, a yellow hourglass means waiting for ingredients, a red bolt means no power, a red flame means no fuel and a red cross means blocked.
  5. Not sure why something stopped? Hover over it (hold a finger on it on a phone) or press E: it says in plain words what it is waiting for and what to do about it.
  6. Press L for the Factory view: the ground dims, every power grid shows in its own colour with how well it is doing, and each machine shows how much it makes a minute.
Tips
  • Start small: one drill, one furnace, one chest. Then copy a working line with V and paste another.
  • The Journal’s Factory tab (J) adds up what the whole farm makes each minute.

Belts & inserters

How things get from A to B: drills, belts, inserters, splitters, sorters and tunnels.

  1. A Mining Drill (Build, Industry) mines the 2×2 patch of ore under it for ever. It hands ore to whatever stands on the tile in front of it: the arrow on the drill shows the front. It needs power.
  2. Conveyor Belts carry things one tile at a time. Drag to lay a line and press R to turn a piece. Items ride to the end and drop into a chest or machine there. A belt that points at nothing is a dead end, and shows a red cross.
  3. An Inserter has an arm: it picks up from the tile behind it and drops on the tile in front. The dot is the pick-up end, the arrow the drop end. It moves things between chests, belts and machines, and needs power.
  4. A Splitter shares a belt three ways, forward, left and right in turn. A Sorter sends the one item you pick (E) straight on and turns everything else off to its sides.
  5. A Tunnel Entrance and Exit, up to 6 tiles apart and facing the same way, let a belt pass under chests, machines and other belts. Click the entrance to check that they are linked.
  6. Chests are the glue: an inserter can fill one or empty one. Open a chest and press Sort & label to make it take only ores, bars or seeds, so a line never gets mixed up.
  7. An Export Chute (Build, Logistics) sells whatever a belt, inserter or drill drops in: 85% of the market price, straight to whoever built it. It never sells gear, tools, pods or crates. press E to sell only one item.
Tips
  • An inserter only takes what the thing in front of it accepts: it waits when a chest is full or a machine has no use for that item.
  • Stuck belts say why when you hover them: a dead end, two belts facing each other, or a full chest at the end.

Power & machines

Drills, inserters and assemblers run on electricity; furnaces burn fuel; sawmills and millstones need neither.

  1. A Wind Turbine (Build, Power) makes free power: 14 to 40 units, as the wind blows. A Coal Generator makes a steady 60 but burns coal, wood or peat. Solar Panels work by day, and Batteries carry a grid through the night.
  2. A Power Pole connects things: it reaches machines within 3 tiles and other poles within 7. Poles that reach each other form one grid, shared by everything wired to it.
  3. A drill uses 20 units, an inserter 3 and an assembler 30. When the grid makes less than its machines want they all slow down; the Factory view (L) shows each grid as a percentage.
  4. A Furnace burns coal, wood or peat: put some in its Fuel slots (E), or let an inserter feed it from a chest that holds coal. A Sawmill and a Millstone need no fuel and no power.
  5. An Assembler builds parts: press E, pick a recipe (gear, wire, circuit…) and feed it the ingredients with inserters from a chest. With no recipe chosen it shows a red cross.
  6. A creature with a standing job at a Furnace, Sawmill, Millstone or Assembler keeps it loaded and emptied from the chests within nine tiles (see Creatures at work).
Tips
  • A machine waiting for power shows a red bolt: check that a pole touches it and that the grid has a generator.
  • Two turbines on a busy grid are safer than one: the wind comes and goes.

Progression tips (47)

Progression tips pop up once each as the game opens out. All of them, in one place.

TipWhat it says
See more of your farmPress + or − to zoom the view in and out (on a phone, pinch). Zoom out to plan your farm, in to look closely.
A chapter is done!It pays out by itself: coins, skill points and gear. The Journal (J) shows the next chapter, bounties and side quests.
BountiesThe Journal (J) lists bounties: small jobs that pay coins, XP and now and then a skill point. New ones keep coming.
Side questsPeople on the island have side quests. Open the Journal (J), the Quests tab, and press Accept. You can pin one to the tracker.
Night is comingMonsters wander out after dark. Light a Campfire or Lantern, swing your weapon with Space and eat or drink to heal.
Gather round the fireStand at a Campfire or Table with a friend, or with your companion beside you, before night falls: a ring fills, and you all earn Hearthside until dawn (+15% XP, faster hearts and energy).
Back on your feetA fall costs half your XP towards the next level and your backpack, unless a friend picks you up (hold E): that is free. Your things wait in a Lost Backpack where you fell: walk back and press E.
Skill points waitingYou have unspent skill points. Press K and spend them: every point makes you a little stronger.
Level 5!From here monsters hit at full strength, so wear armor and keep food or potions handy. Tougher elite monsters show up from level 6.
Level 10!Steel and crystal gear need Steelwork and Crystalwork in the Industry branch (K). Taming, expeditions and bosses are the next big steps.
Level 20!You are strong now. Try an Expedition, call a boss at the Altar, and bring friends: the best gear and skill points come from them.
Copper!Smelt copper ore in a Furnace. Copper bars become wire at the Anvil, and you need wire for Power Poles and circuits.
Iron oreSmelt iron in a Furnace, then the Anvil (Blacksmithing in the skill tree, K) turns the bars into a real pick, swords and armor.
SandMelt sand in a Furnace to make glass. Lanterns, window walls and potions all need glass.
ClayBake clay in a Furnace into bricks: for the Kitchen, brick walls, tile roofs and floors.
Crystals need a better pickCrystal clusters only break with a Golden Pick or better: learn Steelwork (K), then forge one at the Anvil.
Wear some armorAn Iron Helm is 4 iron bars at the Anvil, Iron Mail is 8 bars and 2 cloth. Put it on in the Backpack (I).
CookingA Kitchen turns crops and fish into meals that fill your energy and give lasting buffs. Learn Kitchen Know-How in the Farming branch (K).
Running on emptyWith no energy you move and swing slower. Press F to eat: berries are a quick fix, cooked meals last longer.
Your first machineMachines keep working while you are away. Press E to load them. A creature (P) can keep a furnace stocked for you.
Ore veinsThe dark, cracked patches on the ground are ore veins. A Mining Drill set on one digs it for ever, once it has power. Drills come with Mining Machines in the Industry skills (K).
A mining drill!Drills mine the ore veins under them forever, but they need power: a Wind Turbine, and Power Poles to wire them up.
Power PolesA pole wires machines within 3 tiles and links to other poles up to 7 tiles away. A machine with a blinking bolt has no power.
BeltsDrag to lay a line of belts and press R to turn them. Inserters move items on and off belts, chests and machines.
Ready-made linesNot sure how to wire a factory? Press V and open Starter layouts: a smelter, a flour mill, a mining outpost and more, each placed in one click.
Factory viewPress L (or the belt button under the map) to see how your factory is doing: power grids, what is stuck, and how much each machine makes.
The AssemblerOpen it (E) and pick what it builds: gears, wire, circuits… Then feed it with belts and inserters.
BlueprintsPress V and drag a box round part of your farm to copy it, then paste it anywhere. R turns the paste.
A full ChestA Chest holds 400 items. Build more, or a Steel Chest, which holds 1500 (Logistics in the skill tree).
Label your chestsOpen a Chest and press Sort & label: it then takes only what you choose (all ores, just seeds…) and wears its icon. Inserters and helpers respect that.
A Boss AltarCraft a sigil here (E) from monster drops and use it to call the boss. Bring friends, food and potions, and watch for the red warnings on the ground.
ExpeditionsBuild an Expedition Dock by the water and launch up to four farmers into a rift: waves, a boon between waves and a guardian. Falling costs nothing there.
The BlightThe red marks out at sea on the map (M) are Blight nests. One within 6 plots of your base sends a raid at night. Go out and break it with your weapon: the isle is yours to buy.
A bed far from homeA Bed (Build, Floors & walls) is where you wake up after a fall instead of at home. Put one in an outpost near the nests you mean to break.
The Dread ReachesThe dark violet blocks with a skull on the map (M) are haunted land far out in the wilds. A warden boss guards each one: go in with a party, armor and potions.
Dash!Press Shift (or the DASH button) to roll away with a moment of safety: step out of the red warnings on the ground.
A friend is down!Walk up next to them and hold E: they get back up for free.
TamingCraft pods at a Workbench, then throw one (T) at a wild creature. They are rare and shy: walk up slowly. Stronger pods catch better.
A new friend!Press P to see your creatures. Click one to bring it along, or press Give a job… to put it to work while you are away.
The HatcheryPut two creatures and a few treats in a Hatchery and, after a while, there is an egg. The baby inherits traits from its parents.
Rain!Rain waters your crops, so they grow faster. It also draws wild creatures out of hiding.
WinterCrops grow at less than half speed and the nights are longer. Keep a fire lit and food in your pockets.
WaystonesBuild two or more Waystones, then press E on one to step between them in an instant.
The Fortune WheelThe Fortune Wheel (Build, Special) gives one free spin every day: coins, crates and a jackpot.
The mineThe Mine Shaft leads down to caves as big as the world. Press E to climb down, hold the action key at rock to dig, light a campfire or lantern, and use the ladder to come back up.
Photo modePress F2 to hide the whole HUD for a screenshot, and F2 or Esc to bring it back.
Say helloPress Enter to chat, G then 1 to 8 for an emote, and middle-click to ping a spot for your friends.
